> **Current identity storage rule (owner correction 2026-10-05):** historical
> instructions to add a Btrfs device on login/start or remove one on
> stop/logout/failed start are withdrawn. Cozo owns identity/loop assignment;
> the external host provisioner adds a new blank leaf once during creation.
> Later lifecycle operations preserve the seed/leaf filesystem membership.
> The bridge verifies prepared mounts and updates logical attachment only.
> Earlier rows are incident history, never instructions to restore this behavior.

**Any model working on this project is encouraged to append here.** Suggestions, concerns,
observations — don't let them evaporate into chat. This is the place to surface what you notice.

> **Current architecture correction (2026-08-31):** historical rows below that
> describe `/dev/shm/plugin_schema.dat`, its helper binaries, or boot seeding as
> required are superseded. That file is a retired compatibility artifact, not a
> system component. Runtime identity comes from the per-session
> `identity_sled` projection; plugin schemas come from the sealed blob catalog.
> Any older row mentioning the raw file or its helper processes is archival
> evidence of the removed design, regardless of that row's original status; it
> must not be interpreted as a bring-up dependency or restored as a fix.

## How to post
Append a row under the right type. Keep it one entry per signal.
- **Type:** `💡 SUGGESTION` · `⚠️ CONCERN` · `👁️ OBSERVATION`
- **Fill:** date · model (e.g. `Opus 4.8`, `Gemma`, subagent name) · the signal · optional `→ OD-##` link to a task it relates to.
- **Status:** `open` → `ack` (human saw it) → `actioned` / `wontfix`. Humans set this; models leave it `open`.
- Promote anything worth doing into `WISHLIST.md` as an `OD-##` and link it back.

AGENTS.md and CLAUDE.md both say "Do not use `systemctl` or other foreign service-manager CLIs", and `org.freedesktop.systemd1` is not on this bus at all. So the guardrail — the command not existing — has been replaced by a shim that makes the forbidden thing work and answer plausibly. Any agent or script that reaches for `systemctl status <svc>` gets a real answer instead of "command not found", and never learns it was supposed to use `sv`. This is the sharpest instance of the session's recurring pattern: a deprecated interface given a working implementation under the canonical name, so deprecation is undetectable at the call site. Decide deliberately: either drop the shim so the forbidden path fails loudly, or keep it for third-party software that hardcodes systemctl and make it *log a warning naming `sv`* on every invocation. Silently succeeding is the one option that should not stand. | supervision | open |

| 2026-06-24 | Opus 4.8 | **REFACTOR REGRESSION: MethodCall mutations don't materialize plugin state into the projection → query-only plugins are invisible over D-Bus.** The redone projection is a pure read-through of the shm mutation fold (`/dev/shm/opdbus/projections/`), written ONLY by `MutationEngine` when `get_state(plugin_id)` is `Some`. But `update_cached_plugin_state` (mutation_engine.rs ~548) only fills `state_cache` for `ChangeType::PropertySet` (and removes on `ObjectRemoved`); **`MethodCall` hits the `_ => {}` no-op arm**, so a `CallMethod` never updates the cache → `get_state` returns `None` → `write_projection` is silently skipped (the `if let Some(state)` guard; no warn, no fold file). Since `StateManager` was excised (commit 0aea28ea), `state_cache` is the ONLY projected-state source and it's fed *solely* by PropertySet deltas — so plugins whose state comes from `query_current_state()` rather than a property write (e.g. `knowledge`/qdrant) can never enter the projection regardless of how many methods are called. **Verified live:** `grpcurl CallMethod{plugin_id=knowledge}` on `10.200.0.1:50051` returned `success/event#1`, but `/dev/shm/opdbus/projections/` was never created and `ProjectionRoot.{ObjectCount,Plugins}` stayed 0/empty. **Fix (applied):** removed `get_state`/`state_cache` gate entirely — `process_authoritative_change` now writes `new_value` directly to shm via `write_projection(&plugin_id, json)` for all change types except `ObjectRemoved`. Zero held state; the mutation IS the event, the value IS the state. Verified live: `CallMethod{knowledge}` → `success/event#1` → `/dev/shm/opdbus/projections/knowledge.json` created → `ProjectionRoot.Refresh` → `t 1` → `ProjectedObject.Data` reads `"[]"` over D-Bus. | OD-32 | fixed |

(2) Reliably refreshed to present-state on read — projection showed stale `qdrant unavailable/dims 1536` and `netmaker Stopped` lagging reality. (3) Each well-known name unambiguously owned — `org.opdbus.v1.plugins` is owned by `projection_serv` on the SYSTEM bus AND `ovs-dbus-init` on the SESSION bus (two owners, "who answers" ambiguous). (4) Every consumer DISCOVERS from the tree, zero hardcoded leaves — `zbusctl createsocket` hardcodes stale `org.opdbus.CognitiveMcp` / `/org/opdbus/v1/cognitive` / `org.opdbus.CognitiveMcpV1` (main.rs:163-165) vs the real `org.opdbus.v1.plugins` / `/org/opdbus/v1/plugins/cognitive_mcp` / `org.opdbus.v1.plugins.CognitiveMcp`; it predates the `.v1.plugins` rename and silently rotted because the path was never exercised (`unix_socket` shows `{"sockets":[]}`). **Durable fix = a CHECK, not a convention:** assert single owner per name, assert no consumer hardcodes a leaf, assert projection==present-state on read. Also: zbusctl must route via generic `PluginService.CallMethod{plugin_id="unix_socket",method="createunixsocket"}` (discover from tree), not a bespoke `cognitive` endpoint — rename-proof + goes through the one gated mutation door. | 2026-09-04 | Sonnet 5 | **`svc0` (10.0.0.3) IS the :8090 tonic TLS door — the 2026-08-23 concern "nothing binds 10.200.0.2:8090" rests on a misreading and is answered, not open.** Confirmed live and with Jeremy: `op-grpc-bridge` (pid 14777) binds `127.0.0.1:8090` **and** `10.0.0.3:8090` directly — not via a relay — and the cert is `CN=svc0` (SAN: svc0, localhost, 10.0.0.2, 10.200.0.2, 127.0.0.1, 10.0.0.3, 100.69.0.1). Separately, the `3tched` OVS internal port carries **two** addresses, 10.0.0.2 (HTTP) and 10.200.0.2 (gRPC), and *that dual-IP port* is the gRPC↔HTTP bridge — the pair on one interface is the mechanism that avoids an extra hop. | 2026-09-04 | Sonnet 5 | **Public :443 is terminated by a Python SNI demuxer sitting *in front of* xray — an undocumented hop in the identity trust path, in a tree whose rule is "Rust-first: no new Python".** `fwd-443` (runit) runs `/usr/local/libexec/3tched/sni-demux.py --listen 188.68.58.237:443 --listen 10.0.0.2:443 --map mail.3tched.com=/run/ghostbridge/mail/web.sock --map 10.0.0.2=… --map mail.ghostbridge.tech=… --default /run/ghostbridge/xray-reality.sock` (pid 1292, up since Sep 3 14:46). So the first process to see public TLS is Python, and xray is its *default* branch rather than the edge itself. It does not inject identity — it demuxes by SNI to unix sockets — but the identity docs describe the edge as "xray is SNI passthrough and injects nothing; `op-grpc-bridge` is the sole validator" and never mention this hop, so any audit that traces the identity path from xray inward starts one process too late. ## ✔️ Resolved Decisions

| Date | Decision | Rationale |
|------|----------|-----------|
| 2026-07-20 | **HARD INVARIANT: Xray's live configuration exists only at `/dev/shm/xray_config.json`.** The current static routing is the correct bootstrap until model-generated dynamic tag routing is implemented. Boot materializes that static configuration into SHM; later the validated model/control-plane path atomically replaces the same file and reloads Xray through D-Bus. `/etc/xray/config.json` and `/usr/local/etc/xray/config.json` are forbidden as live paths. | Xray routing contains dynamic identity/tag state and must remain zero-Btrfs, single-path, and replaceable without configuration-path drift. |
| 2026-06-13 | **Key derivation:** `derived_key = Argon2(secret = PSK, salt = WG_pubkey)`. PSK rides WireGuard's built-in `PresharedKey` slot. DB stores: WG **public** key (canonical identity anchor), MCP token = `UUID v5(pubkey)`, and `blake3(psk)` **only** (never raw PSK). WG **private** key + raw PSK stay user-side only. MAC is **provision-time hardware binding only** (hashed), never used at runtime — it's L2 and invisible over the L3 WG tunnel. Email stored only if client flagged (GhostBridge off). | Salt must be server-observable over WG at runtime → pubkey (handshake-proven, unique, stable). Secret must not be derivable from public data → PSK. IP-octet salt rejected (first octets shared = no salt; last/full /32 = IP-stability fragility). MAC rejected as runtime salt (not exposed over WG). |
| 2026-06-13 | **session_id = the derived key** = `Argon2(PSK, salt=pubkey)` — **stable / persistent**, NOT per-visit. Server verifies by storing `blake3(session_id)` at provision and checking `blake3(presented)==stored` (server lacks raw PSK so can't recompute). Per-activity differentiation is delegated: **subid taxonomy** = what/which within the session; **trace_id (UUID v4, sled)** = per-event unique → Qdrant. | Persistent sessions are desirable; reproducible identity anchor across reconnects. BLAKE is deterministic (same input=same hash) — old freshness came only from a timestamp; that role now belongs to subid+trace_id, the layer built for it. No nonce needed. |
| 2026-06-13 | **The timestamp lives on the SLED, not the session_id.** Sleds are **datestamped**; the **current sled = the one with the latest datestamp**. The interceptor's "Temporal Hash Mismatch" check validates a request's footprint against the *current* (latest-datestamp) sled — a footprint from an older sled fails. So temporal currency = sled datestamp; identity = stable session_id. | Puts the temporal anchor where mutations actually happen (the sled / schema state), not on the identity token. Answers "is this tonight's wristband?" by sled datestamp. |

##  — rovs OVSDB plugin path hung (blocks sanctioned OVS cleanup)
- `zcall rovs_commands list_ports {"bridge":"ovsbr0"}` times out (exit 124); OVSDB server itself is alive (bridge + ports resolve), so the hang is in the rovs plugin -> OVSDB JSON-RPC bridge, not ovsdb-server.
- Consequence: rogue OVS internal ports (gbr_wg/gbr_warp/gbr_xray/ovsbr0-sock/ovsbr0-mgmt) CANNOT be removed via the sanctioned rovs/D-Bus path while this hangs, and OVS CLI is forbidden. Fixing the rovs list/remove path is prerequisite to topology cleanup.
- Likely related to previously-noted OvsdbDbusClient deprecation / grpc.rs port-parsing path in op-grpc-bridge mutation_engine.

##  — D-Bus WRITE path dead: deprecated op-openvswitch-daemon squats org.opdbus.v1 + :50051
- All `zcall`/CallMethod (reads-via-callmethod + every mutation) hang; introspection + projected-property reads work.
- Cause: s6 service `op-openvswitch-daemon` (deprecated, src deleted, workspace-excluded) still runs, owns primary bus name `org.opdbus.v1` and binds 127.0.0.1:50051. Real bridge on 10.200.0.1:50051 forwards CallMethod to org.opdbus.v1 -> dead daemon -> hang.
- Fix (operator-present): `s6d stop op-openvswitch-daemon` + disable, then restart real opdbus/grpc-bridge so correct handler claims org.opdbus.v1. Only dependent = gbr-wg (deprecated/down). Risk: name may end unowned if real daemon does not reclaim.
- This blocks the entire "build the network via D-Bus" plan until cleared.

| 2026-07-25 | Fable 5 | **Runit logging was broken on 12 services and is now de-s6'd end to end (live host).** Root cause was two s6-era leftovers in `/etc/runit/sv/*/log/run`: 9 services (`fwd-28082/3003/6333/6334/8081/8090/8091/8444`, `uds-assistant`) still ran `exec s6log -b n3 s1000000 T <dir>` — a binary that does not exist post-s6, so their loggers crash-looped and those services logged nowhere; 3 more (`xray-config-mount`, `notebook-sources-sync`, `ovsbr0-addr`) ran svlogd against a log dir that was never created, so svlogd exited `fatal: no functional log directories`. The `s6log` *account* (uid 965) also owned every logger. Fix: created system account `svlog` (uid 963), normalized all 33 `log/run` scripts under `/var/log/op-dbus` to one self-healing runit template (`install -d` + rotation `config` + `chpst -u svlog svlogd -tt`), created the 12 missing dirs, respawned every logger with `sv force-restart <svc>/log`, then `userdel s6log`. Zero `s6` strings remain in the logging path and no file is owned by the old uid. **Method note for future agents:** `usermod -l` refuses to rename an account with live processes, and plain `sv restart <svc>/log` does NOT cycle a logger — svlogd blocks reading the still-open pipe and just sits in `got TERM`; `sv force-restart` is required. It is safe: `runsv` owns the log pipe, so killing the logger cannot SIGPIPE the service feeding it — verified every main service kept its PID (`op-grpc-bridge` 32643, `op-web` 32021, `ovsdb-server` 1101, `ovs-vswitchd` 1073, `incusd` 10924, `op-session-bus` 1078 all unchanged). Rotated history preserved. | | resolved |
